p = np.exp(z)
S = p.sum()
p = p / S  # since objective scale invariant, softmax normalization not necessary but helps.
if not finite...
c = _self_conv(p)
d = n * c  # since S=1
M = len(d)
# top k active
idx = np.argpartition(d, M-k)[M-k:]

dmax=dact.max()
if ...
z2 = beta*(dact-dmax)
e=np.exp(z2)

val=2*L
# compute corr with w over full convolution length M
corr = _cross_corr(p, w_full)  # length n
grad_p = 4.0 * n * corr  # derivative of val w.r.t p (as if p unconstrained)
# project to simplex tangent: g_y = p*(grad_p - dot(p,grad_p))
dot = float(p @ grad_p)
g = p * (grad_p - dot)
# maybe add tiny regularization to avoid huge y? Not needed. Could add lambda*y to keep bounded? y can drift; softmax only depends on differences. L-BFGS may drift without affecting objective. Let's refine LSE log-domain objective. We must be careful with top-k selection when M small. For M=2n-1.
```
def _lse_obj_grad_logy(y, beta, k):
    n = int(len(y))
    M = 2*n-1
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(y)): return 1e100, zeros
    z = y - np.max(y)
    p = np.exp(z)
    s = p.sum()
    if not finite or s<1e-300: p uniform, g zeros
    p /= s
    c = _self_conv(p)
    d = c * float(n)  # since sum=1
    d = np.nan_to_num(d,...)
    if k >= M:
        idx = np.arange(M); dact=d
    else:
        # use argpartition; if d includes zeros? okay
        idx = np.argpartition(d, M-k)[M-k:]  # if k=M, M-k=0? handle
        dact = d[idx]
    dmax = dact.max()
    if dmax <= 0: return ...
    z2 = beta*(dact - dmax)
    e = np.exp(z2)
    se = e.sum()
    if se <=0: weights uniform among active? 
    w = e/se
    L = dmax + np.log(max(se,1e-300))/beta
    val = 2.0*L
    # Full weights length M to compute corr
    wfull = np.zeros(M)
    wfull[idx] = w
    corr = _cross_corr(p, wfull)
    grad_p = 4.0 * float(n) * corr
    dot = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))
    g = p * (grad_p - dot)
    # optional add small regularization to y: 1e-8 * y_center? If y not centered, could.
    # Also if g nonfinite set 0.
    g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    # Clip g to avoid overflow in BFGS
    maxg = np.max(np.abs(g))
    if maxg > 1e6: g *= 1e6/maxg
    return val, g
```

Pseudo:
```
def _lse_obj(x, beta, eps, reg):
    n = len(x)
    if not finite: ...
    z = x - max(x)
    # maybe don't clip? exp underflow ok.
    ps = np.exp(z)
    s = ps.sum()
    if s <=0 or not finite: return large, zeros
    ps /= s
    p = (1-eps)*ps + eps / n
    c = _self_conv(p)
    d = c * n
    dmax = d.max()
    if dmax <=0 ...
    e = np.exp(beta*(d-dmax))
    se = e.sum()
    if se<=0: w=np.ones(M)/M; L=dmax
    else: w=e/se; L=dmax+log(se)/beta
    val=2*L
    corr = _cross_corr(p, w)
    gp = 4.0*n*corr
    dot = ps @ gp  # not p? Chain uses ps.
    g = (1-eps)*ps*(gp - dot*ps)
    if reg:
       val += reg * float(np.dot(x,x))? But x may not centered; reg on x.
       g += 2*reg*x
    return val,g
```
Note if we use p in gp, but derivative of val wrt p. The mapping p(ps) linear: p=(1-eps)ps+eps/n, dp/dps=(1-eps)I. So gp wrt p times (1-eps). Good.
But objective val depends on p, not ps directly; however p and ps one-to-one. Good.

Parent loading:
```
def _load_parent():
    h = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    if h is None: return None
    p = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.clip(p,0,None)
    if p.sum()<1e-12: return None
    return p/p.sum()
```

Upsample:
```
def _interp(a, m):
    a=np.asarray(a); n=len(a)
    if n==1: return np.full(m,a[0])
    x0=np.linspace(0,1,n); x1=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    return np.interp(x1,x0,a)
```

- Gaussian around center: p=exp(-((x-0.5)^2)/(2σ²)) for σ 0.1-0.4. But symmetric may high center; still maybe.
- asymmetric Gaussian: p=exp(-((x-μ)^2)/(2σ²)) with μ not 0.5, e.g., 0.3,0.7, σ 0.1,0.2,0.3.
- power laws: p=(x+eps)^α*(1-x+eps)^β, α,β in {-2,-1,-0.5,0,0.5,1,2,3}. Normalize.
- Fourier random: Choose random coefficients with decay: p=1 + Σ a_m cos(2π m x + φ). To ensure positive, start base 0.5 + sum small. Or use `spectral_init` previous but with real? We can implement random real trigonometric:

for m in 1..terms: p += amp*(0.5/m)*cos(2π m x + phase)

    p = _norm(p0)
    best_p = p; best_sc = _score(p)
    print start
    # stages: time fractions and beta

    total_time = max(20, deadline - now - 10)
    cum=0
    for beta, frac in stages:
       stage_end = start + (cum+frac)*total_time
       stage_end=min(stage_end, deadline-10)
       budget = stage_end - now
       if budget<5 break
       # maybe split into 2 calls if large budget to update best

Exact subgradient (simplex projection) can improve after LSE. Let's implement robust `_project_simplex(v)`:
Top-level. For n large, sort O(n log n). We'll use if few iterations.
```
def _project_simplex(v):
    n=len(v)
    if n==0: return v
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,n+1))-1>0)[0]
    if len(rho)==0: return np.zeros(n)
    alphav=(cssv[rho[-1]]-1)/ (rho[-1]+1)
    return np.maximum(v-alphav,0)
```
But objective sum=1; projection returns sum1.

Subgradient exact in p simplex using tanget gradient and projection:
```
def _subgrad_polish(p, deadline, max_iters=50, active_tol=1e-6, steps=...):
    p=_norm(p); best=...
    for it:
       c,d,dmax
       # active weights: maybe softmax high beta over all d? For exact max, use top within tol. But if only one max, okay.
       active = d >= dmax - max(1e-5, 1e-6*dmax)
       if count<16? add top 16 to smooth.
       w=zeros; w[active]=1/count
       corr = _cross_corr(p,w)
       gp=4*n*corr
       # projected gradient direction: q = p - eta*gp then project. But gp magnitude? choose eta adaptive.
       # Since we restrict sum, better use tangent g_t=gp - mean(gp); direction projected.
       for eta in scales:
           q = p - eta*gp
           q = _project_simplex(q)
           sc=_score(q)
           choose best
       if no improve break
       p=best
```

Now, consider using `scipy.optimize.minimize` on y with LSE full at low beta. The objective is smooth but nonconvex. To escape local, perturb p with smooth noise. Implement `_perturb(p, rng, sigma)`:
- Generate Gaussian noise, smooth in frequency: keep low freq. For n large, FFT noise, multiply by lowpass window e^{-k^2/width}, random phase? Simpler, kernel convolution. But for sine shape, low-freq perturb good. Use FFT:
```
def _smooth_perturb_fft(p, rng, sigma=0.1):
    n=len(p)
    N=next_pow2(n)
    noise=np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), N)
    freqs=np.fft.rfftfreq(N)*N
    window=np.exp(-(freqs**2)/(2*(n/10)**2))
    noise=np.fft.irfft(noise*window, N)[:n]
    # normalize noise zero mean std1
    noise -= noise.mean(); noise/=std+eps
    q=p + sigma*noise
    q=np.clip(q,0,None); normalize
```
This adds low-frequency perturbations. Strength schedule small (0.03-0.1). Use after each trajectory to restart.

But support edges matter; rolling creates zeros at edges, maybe bad. Not.

Maybe "stretch/squash" x coordinate: resample p to m and back? Not.

Let's write functions mentally.

`_normalize(a, target=1.0)`:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size==0: return np.array([1.0])
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    s=a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12:
        return np.full(a.size, target/a.size)
    return a*(target/s)
```

`_score(a)`:
```
def _score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    a=np.nan_to_num(...)
    a=np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    S=a.sum()
    if S<1e-12: return np.inf
    c=_self_conv(a)
    m=float(c.max()) if c.size else 0
    if not finite: return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*m/(S*S))
```
Since self_conv uses normalized? No, any.

`_self_conv(a)`: use float64. If n=1 return [a0^2]. N next pow2 2n-1. Rfft. Square magnitude? Wait convolution of a with itself, not autocorr. For real a, rfft(a)*rfft(a) (not conjugate) gives DFT of linear convolution? Yes, DFT of convolution is product of DFTs (no conjugate). For real but not symmetric, product not magnitude squared. Need use A*A, not A*np.conj(A). Previous code did A * A. Good. Then irfft. Ensure small negative from rounding set 0? Since true convolution nonnegative if a nonnegative, but FFT errors can negative tiny. Use `np.maximum(out,0)`. Good.
```
A=np.fft.rfft(a,N); out=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)[:M]; out=np.nan_to_num(out); np.maximum(out,0,out)
```

`_cross_corr(a,w)`: Need convolve reversed a with w, then select. Use rfft(a[::-1],N)*rfft(w,N), irfft. But w length M=2n-1. N next pow2 len(rev)+len(w)-1 =3n-2. If w is all zeros? okay. Select indices n-1:2n-1. Because full length L=n+M-1=3n-2. Need result length n. Let's verify with previous. For n=2, a_rev length2, w length3, full4 indices1:3 length2. Good.
```
def _cross_corr(a,w):
    n=a.size; m=w.size
    if n==0 or m==0: return zeros
    L=n+m-1; N=_next_pow2(L)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a[::-1],N); W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    full=np.fft.irfft(A*W,N)[:L]
    out=full[n-1:n-1+n]
    return np.nan_to_num(out)
```
This out matches half gradient? For w length M full conv. Good. Note if w length M, n+m-1=3n-2 (not 3n-1? n+2n-1-1=3n-2). Good.

`_p_from_y(y, eps)`:
```
def _p_from_y(y, eps):
    n=len(y)
    z=y-np.max(y)
    ps=np.exp(z)
    s=ps.sum()
    if s<=0 or not finite: ps=np.ones(n)/n
    else: ps/=s
    p=(1.0-eps)*ps + eps/n
    # ensure sum exactly 1
    p=p/p.sum()
    return p
```
If eps=0? We'll use >0. If y all -inf? not.

`_p_to_y(p, eps)`:
```
def _p_to_y(p, eps):
    floor = max(1e-300, eps/(1-eps)/max(1,p.size)) maybe
    p=np.clip(p,floor,None); p/=p.sum()
    ps=(p - eps/n)/(1-eps)
    ps=np.clip(ps,1e-300,None)
    y=np.log(ps)
    y-=np.mean(y)
    return y
```
If p < eps/n, ps negative; floor prevents. Use eps small. If eps=1e-9, eps/n ~1.5e-14. If p has zeros, p floor may be >eps/n? We set floor = eps/( (1-eps)*n ) = ~1.5e-14, then p-eps/n =0 => ps tiny. Use clip.

`_lse_obj(x, beta, eps, reg)`:
Need be fast. Use local variables, avoid repeated global lookups? okay.
```
def _lse_obj(x, beta, eps, reg):
    n=int(x.size)
    if n==0: return 1e100, np.array(0.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)): return 1e100, np.zeros(n)
    z=x-np.max(x)
    ps=np.exp(z)
    s=ps.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n)
    ps=s? if s finite: ps/=s else uniform
    p=(1-eps)*ps + eps/n
    # p sum 1
    c=_self_conv(p)
    M=c.size
    d=c*float(n)
    # d should be nonnegative; max
    dmax=float(np.max(d)) if M else 0
    if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax<=0:
         return 1e100, zeros
    with np.errstate(under='ignore', over='ignore'):
        e=np.exp(beta*(d-dmax))
    se=float(e.sum())
    if not finite or se<=0:
        w=np.full(M,1.0/M)
        L=dmax
    else:
        w=e/se
        L=dmax + np.log(se)/float(beta)
    val=2.0*L
    corr=_cross_corr(p,w)
    gp=4.0*float(n)*corr
    dot=float(np.dot(ps,gp))
    g=(1.0-eps)*ps*(gp - dot*ps)
    if reg !=0:
       val += reg*float(np.dot(x,x))
       g = g + (2.0*reg)*x
    g=np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    # clip
    mg=float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if n else 0
    if mg > 1e6:
        g *= 1e6/mg
    return float(val), g
```
Potential issue: `_self_conv(p)` may have tiny negative after max? we max. It performs FFT, costly; `_cross_corr` second FFT. Good.

But derivative `gp` for objective val computed at p; LSE weights w from d. If beta large, w peaked. Good.

Reg uses x raw; if x large due to constant shift, g includes 2reg x. But `g` already projected sum zero? Add reg breaks sum zero, making constant direction nonzero; good to pull x to zero. But the mapping p depends only differences; reg objective on x may cause L-BFGS to move constant until sum x=0. It also adds gradient along p directions? Same x plus constant? The p derivative unaffected by constant, but reg gradient constant component 2reg*const. If x has mean m, reg gradient 2reg m along all entries, not projected; BFGS will reduce mean. Good. But if x has large mean 100, reg gradient 2e-10*100=2e-8, small. Fine.

However objective val includes `reg*dot(x,x)`, which is not invariant under y shift; the minimizer of full objective will bias y toward zero in Euclidean norm. This may distort p slightly because for a given p, the y with smallest norm is the centered one (mean zero). Since p only differences, the minimal reg y for p is mean-zero. BFGS effectively uses that. Good, no distortion.

Can we improve by using topK to reduce exp? Full okay. But if beta high and d-dmax range large, e underflows all but max; se=1, w has 1 at max. LSE value dmax. Gradient only max. This equals exact max subgradient. Good.

However L-BFGS may struggle when weights concentrate on a single coordinate due to non-differentiability; high beta stage should be final short. We'll schedule high beta last and then subgradient.

def _run_lbfgs(p0, beta, time_limit, maxfun_cap, eps, reg):
    p0=_normalize(p0)
    best_p=p0.copy(); best_sc=_score(p0)
    n=len(p0)
    if time_limit < 3: return best_sc,best_p
    y=_p_to_y(p0, eps)
    # estimate
    est = 0.006*max(1.0, n/16384.0) + 0.002
    mf = int(0.85*time_limit/est)
    mf = max(40, min(mf, maxfun_cap, 5000))
    maxiter=max(20, mf//3)
    args=(beta,eps,reg)
    try:
       res=minimize(_lse_obj, y, method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, args=args,
                    options={'maxiter':maxiter,'maxfun':mf,'ftol':1e-15,'gtol':1e-10,'maxls':30})
       if hasattr(res,'x') and res.x is not None:
           p_new=_p_from_y(res.x, eps)
           p_new=_normalize(p_new)
           sc_new=_score(p_new)
           if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < best_sc:
               best_p=p_new; best_sc=sc_new
           # even if not better, maybe return p_new? We can return best_p to caller; but caller may want p_new for homotopy.
           # return both? we can return p_new as current but best score? Modify return (best_sc, p_best, p_current)
    except: pass
    return best_sc,best_p
```

def _make_candidates(n, parent, rng):
    cands=[]
    if parent is not None:
       p=_normalize(_interp(parent,n)); cands.append(('parent',p))
       p=_normalize(_interp(parent[::-1],n)); cands.append(('parent_rev',p))
    else:
       cands.append(('uniform', _normalize(np.ones(n))))
    # sine1 alphas
    for alpha in (0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
       x=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)
       p=1.0 + alpha*np.sin(2*np.pi*x)
       p=np.clip(p,0,None); if p.sum()>0: cands.append((f'sine{alpha}', _normalize(p)))
       # phase shift 0.25 maybe 1+alpha sin(2πx + φ)
    for shift in (0.0,0.1,0.2, -0.1):
       alpha=0.6
       p=1+alpha*np.sin(2*np.pi*(x+shift)); clip; normalize
    # sine multi deterministic
    p=1 + 0.6*sin(2πx) + 0.2*sin(4πx) + 0.1*sin(6πx); clip; normalize
    p=1 + 0.5*sin(2πx) - 0.25*sin(4πx); ...
    # beta/power: for aa in (0,0.5,1,2,3) bb? p=(x*eps+? )
    for aa,bb in [(1,1),(2,1),(0.5,2),(3,0.5),(0,3),(3,0)]:
         y=np.linspace(0,1,n,endpoint=False)+1e-6
         p=(y**aa)*((1-y+1e-6)**bb) # if aa=0 okay
         normalize
    # exponential: for lam in (-2,-1,-0.5,0.5,1,2)
         p=np.exp(lam*(x-0.5)); normalize
    # gauss: for mu in (0.2,0.35,0.5,0.65,0.8), sig in (0.1,0.2,0.3)
    # random smooth via rng maybe few
    return cands
```

Random smooth:
```
def _random_smooth_init(n, rng, strength=1.0):
    z=rng.standard_normal(n)
    # FFT lowpass
    N=_next_pow2(n)
    Z=np.fft.rfft(z,N)
    f=np.fft.rfftfreq(N)*N
    cutoff=n/5
    W=np.exp(-(f/cutoff)**2)
    z=np.fft.irfft(Z*W,N)[:n]
    z-=z.mean(); z/=(z.std()+1e-12)
    p=np.exp(strength*z)
    return _normalize(p)
```
This creates log-normal smoothed random positive.

Now, trajectory function with stages. Let's implement `_trajectory_lbfgs(p0, stage_deadline, label)`.
Need print. It will use `_run_lbfgs`.
```
def _trajectory_lbfgs(p0, deadline, label):
    t_start_cur=time.monotonic()
    p_cur=_normalize(p0)
    best_p=p_cur.copy(); best_sc=_score(p_cur)
    print(f"  [{label}] start n={len(p_cur)} score={best_sc:.8f}")
    stages = [(50,0.08),(150,0.10),(400,0.12),(1000,0.14),(2500,0.18),(6000,0.20),(15000,0.18)]
    available=max(10.0, deadline - t_start_cur - 5.0)
    cum=0; maxfun_cap = 2500 if n<=8192 else 1200 if n<=16384 else 700
    for beta,frac in stages:
       cum += frac
       stage_end = t_start_cur + cum*available
       if stage_end > deadline - 3: stage_end = deadline -3
       budget=stage_end - time.monotonic()
       if budget < 4: break
       n_calls=1 if budget<20 else (2 if budget<80 else 3)
       per=budget/n_calls
       for ci in range(n_calls):
          if time.monotonic() > stage_end-2: break
          sc, bp, cp = _run_lbfgs(p_cur, beta, per, maxfun_cap, 1e-9, 1e-12)
          if sc < best_sc:
             best_sc=sc; best_p=bp; print
          # choose next current
          cur_score = _score(cp) if cp is not None else np.inf
          if (np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score <= best_sc*1.003 + 1e-6):
              p_cur=cp
          else:
              p_cur=best_p
    # subgrad polish short?
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 8:
       sc_sg, p_sg = _subgrad_polish(p_cur, deadline-5, max_iters=15)
       if sc_sg<best_sc: update
    return best_sc,best_p
```
But subgradient within each trajectory may be costly and duplicate final. Maybe only final global. Skip in trajectory? Could do if time.

Beta 15000 final. For high n, d~O1; enough.

_run_lbfgs return current p maybe p_new. If p_new score worse, p_cur stays best. But homotopy might want p_new. We use tolerance 0.3%. Good.

Could `_run_lbfgs` return `res.x` p even if true score worse; maybe for next beta. The tolerance decides. If it worsens >0.3%, reset to best to avoid drift. Good.

Subgradient polish top-level. Let's design carefully.
```
def _subgrad_polish(p0, deadline, max_iters=15):
    p=_normalize(p0); best_p=p; best_sc=_score(p)
    n=len(p); M=2*n-1
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
       c=_self_conv(p); d=c*n; dmax=float(d.max())
       if not finite: break
       # active top maybe 16 and near max
       if M > 0:
           tol = max(1e-7, 1e-5*dmax)
           active = d >= dmax - tol
           if active.sum() < 16:
               idx = np.argpartition(d, min(M,16)-1)[-min(M,16):]
               active = np.zeros(M,bool); active[idx]=True
           else:
               # limit to top 64 if too many? near max count maybe many. Use all active.
           cnt=int(active.sum())
           w=np.zeros(M); w[active]=1.0/cnt
       corr=_cross_corr(p,w)
       gp=4.0*float(n)*corr
       # gradient norm scaled
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(gp))) if n else 0
       if gmax <=0: break
       eta_scales=(0.0002,0.001,0.005,0.02,0.1,0.5)
       best_step=None; best_step_sc=best_sc
       for s in eta_scales:
           eta=s/gmax
           q=p - eta*gp
           q=_project_simplex(q)
           sc=_score(q)
           if sc < best_step_sc:
               best_step_sc=sc; best_step=q.copy()
       if best_step is None: break
       if best_step_sc < best_sc:
           best_sc=best_step_sc; best_p=best_step
           p=best_step
       else:
           # maybe try smaller scale? If no improve break
           break
    return best_sc,best_p
```
But if `active` huge, w uniform over many near max; gradient maybe small. `gmax` scale okay. Project simplex sort each eval. 6 score evals per iter. max_iters 15 ->90 scores. For n=32768, each score ~0.005? 0.45s, okay. For 8192, fast. Good.

Could improve step scales by using `eta = 1/(n * gmax)`? gp magnitude maybe 4n*corr. Corr ~? For p ~1/n, w ~1/K, corr ~1/(K n). gp~4/K. gmax O1. Projection q=p - eta gp; p entries ~1/n. If eta 0.1, change ~0.1 could make negatives. Projection handles. Scales up to 0.5 okay. But maybe need larger scale to move? Use scales `(0.001,0.005,0.02,0.1,0.5,2.0,10.0)`. If gmax small, eta large. We'll use `(0.001,0.005,0.02,0.1,0.5,2.0)`.

Project simplex accuracy: If q has large values, sort O(n). Fine.

def _zero_small_polish(p):
   p=_normalize(p); best=...

       q=p.copy(); q[q<thr]=0; q=_normalize(q)
       sc=_score(q); if sc<best: update
   return best
```

Detail `_next_pow2`:
```
def _next_pow2(m):
    m=int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```
If m=1, (0).bit_length()=0 ->1. Good.

`_self_conv`:
```
def _self_conv(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.empty(0,dtype=np.float64)
    if n==1:
       return np.array([a[0]*a[0]],dtype=np.float64)
    M=2*n-1
    N=_next_pow2(M)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(A*A,N)
    out=out[:M]
    out=np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(out,0.0,out)
    return out
```
For A*A complex, product may have tiny imaginary? rfft returns complex; A*A not conjugate, irfft assumes Hermitian? Does irfft input need Hermitian? The product A*A of rfft of real sequence is not necessarily Hermitian? Wait! Critical: For linear convolution of real sequences via FFT, we compute `np.fft.rfft(a, N) * np.fft.rfft(a, N)`, but irfft expects a conjugate-symmetric full spectrum corresponding to real output. The product of two real-signal DFTs: Is it Hermitian? For convolution of real sequence with itself (not autocorr), the result is real (convolution of real sequences). The full DFT of real convolution is A(ω)^2. Since the time sequence is real, its DFT must be Hermitian: X(-ω)=conj(X(ω)). Is A(ω)^2 a DFT of a real sequence? Yes, convolution of real sequences is real. Does A(ω)^2 satisfy Hermitian? A(-ω)=conj(A(ω)), so A(-ω)^2 = conj(A(ω))^2 = (A(ω)^2)^*. Thus yes it is Hermitian. Good. rfft stores only nonnegative frequencies; product at positive frequencies is A_k^2. The negative counterpart is conjugate. irfft reconstructs. Good.

`_cross_corr`: We compute conv of a_rev (real) and w (real) via FFT product A*W. Both real -> convolution real. Product is A(ω)W(ω), full DFT of convolution. Is it Hermitian? A(-)=conj(A), W(-)=conj(W), product conjugate. Good. irfft okay.

But selecting `full[n-1:2*n-1]` length n. In previous, for n=2, M=3, L=4, N=4? `_next_pow2(4)=4`. full len4. select index1:3 length2. Good. For general, L=3n-2. Need N>=L; next pow2. full len N, take first L. select `n-1` to `n-1+n` = 2n-1, which is less than L? L=3n-2, 2n-1 <= 3n-2 for n>=1. Good.

Test gradient formula with w full length M. `_cross_corr` returns length n. Good.

`_normalize` target default 1.0. If a has size large, `target / a.size` if sum zero. Good.

`_interp`:
```
def _interp(a,m):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if m<=0: return a
    if n==0: return np.ones(m)
    if n==m: return a.copy()
    if n==1: return np.full(m,a[0])
    x0=np.linspace(0,1,n)
    x1=np.linspace(0,1,m)
    return np.interp(x1,x0,a)
```
This maps endpoints inclusive, so sequence at x=1 for last. For convolution support, including both endpoints duplicated? Using linspace endpoint=True means i=0..n-1 uniform. Good. Could use endpoint=False to match discrete i/n? The sequence positions 0..n-1; interpolation for upsample should map old indices 0..n-1 to new 0..m-1. `np.linspace(0,1,n)` maps last old to 1, last new to 1. This is fine (edge shared). The density scaling sum normalized. Good.

`_p_to_y`: Need not use `eps`? Let's write robust:
```
def _p_to_y(p, eps):
    p=_normalize(p)
    n=p.size
    lower = max(1e-300, eps/((1-eps)*n)*0.5 + 1e-300)
    p=np.maximum(p, lower)
    p=p/p.sum()
    ps=(p - eps/n)/(1-eps)
    ps=np.maximum(ps, 1e-300)
    y=np.log(ps)
    y-=np.mean(y)
    return y
```
If eps close 1? no.

Unique check helper `_similar(a,b,threshold=0.999)`:

def _similar(a,b,threshold=0.999):
    a=_normalize(a); b=_normalize(b)
    if a.size != b.size:
       if a.size < b.size: a=_interp(a,b.size)
       else: b=_interp(b,a.size)
    a=_normalize(a); b=_normalize(b)
    da=a.sum(); db=b.sum(); if da<... return False
    return float(np.dot(a,b,where?)) / np.sqrt(da*db) > threshold
```

`_shape_candidates`: Need avoid too many names. Use string concatenation. For alpha floats, `('sine1_'+str(alpha))`. For shifts, `('sine'+str(shift))`.
Implementation:
```
def _shape_candidates(n, rng, max_random=3):
    arr=[]
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    two_pi_x=2.0*np.pi*x
    for alpha in (0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9):
       p=1.0 + alpha*np.sin(two_pi_x)
       p=np.clip(p,1e-10,None)
       arr.append(('sine1_'+str(alpha), _normalize(p)))
    for shift in (-0.2,-0.1,0.1,0.2):
       p=1.0 + 0.7*np.sin(two_pi_x + 2.0*np.pi*shift)
       p=np.clip(p,1e-10,None)
       arr.append(('sines'+str(shift), _normalize(p)))
    coeff=[(0.7,0.0,0.0),(0.6,0.25,0.1),(0.5,0.3,0.2),(0.4,0.45,0.25),(0.3,0.55,0.35),(0.5,-0.3,0.1)]
    for i,(a1,a2,a3) in enumerate(coeff):
       p=1 + a1*np.sin(two_pi_x) + a2*np.sin(4*np.pi*x) + a3*np.sin(6*np.pi*x)
       p=np.clip(p,1e-10,None)
       arr.append(('sinem'+str(i), _normalize(p)))
    for lam in (-2.0,-1.5,-1.0,-0.5,0.0,0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0):
       p=np.exp(lam*(x-0.5))
       arr.append(('exp'+str(lam), _normalize(p)))
    beta_vals=[(1.0,1.0),(2.0,1.0),(1.0,2.0),(3.0,1.0),(1.0,3.0),(0.5,1.0),(1.0,0.5),(0.0,2.0),(2.0,0.0),(4.0,1.0),(1.0,4.0)]
    yb=x+1e-6; qb=1.0-x+1e-6
    for i,(aa,bb) in enumerate(beta_vals):
       if aa == 0.0: fa=1.0
       else: fa=np.power(yb, aa)
       if bb == 0.0: fb=1.0
       else: fb=np.power(qb, bb)
       p=fa*fb
       p=np.clip(p,1e-10,None)
       arr.append(('beta'+str(i), _normalize(p)))
    for mu in (0.25,0.35,0.65,0.75):
       for sig in (0.12,0.2,0.3):
          p=np.exp(-((x-mu)**2)/(2.0*sig*sig))
          arr.append(('gauss_%s_%s'%(mu,sig), _normalize(p)))
    for r in range(max_random):
       arr.append(('rand%d'%r, _random_smooth_init(n,rng)))
    return arr
```
`%s` with floats okay. Could many: sine1 4, shifts4, multi6, exp9,beta11,gauss12,rand3 total49. Fine.

`_random_smooth_init` as above. Need no closures.

       if sc < local_best_sc: update
       # update base_p with new local best for next trajectories
       local_base = local_best_p if len==n else interp

def _zero_small_polish(p):
   p=_normalize(p); best=p; best_sc=_score(p)
   n=p.size
   for thr in (1e-14,1e-12,1e-10):
      q=p.copy(); q[q<thr]=0.0
      if q.sum()<=0: continue
      q=q/q.sum()

      if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=q
   return best_sc,best

`_smooth_perturb`:
```
def _smooth_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.1):
    p=_normalize(p); n=p.size; N=_next_pow2(n)
    z=np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), N)
    f=np.fft.rfftfreq(N)*N
    cutoff=max(1.0, n/8.0)
    W=np.exp(-(f/cutoff)**2)
    noise=np.fft.irfft(z*W, N)[:n]
    noise -= noise.mean()
    sd=noise.std()
    if sd>1e-12: noise /= sd
    q=p + strength*noise
    return _normalize(q)
```
If strength 0.2. Good.

Now, `_trajectory_lbfgs` stages and subgrad. Let's define with parameters to avoid nested:
```
def _trajectory_lbfgs(p0, stage_deadline, label):
    p_cur=_normalize(p0)
    best_p=p_cur.copy(); best_sc=_score(p_cur)
    t0=time.monotonic()
    n=len(p_cur)
    maxfun_cap = 3000 if n<=8192 else (1500 if n<=16384 else 800)
    stages=[(50,0.08),(150,0.10),(400,0.12),(1000,0.14),(2500,0.18),(6000,0.20),(15000,0.18)]
    available=max(10.0, stage_deadline - t0 - 5.0)
    cum=0
    for beta,frac in stages:
       cum += frac
       stage_end = t0 + cum*available
       if stage_end > stage_deadline - 3: stage_end=stage_deadline -3
       budget=stage_end - time.monotonic()
       if budget < 4: break
       n_calls=1
       if budget > 60: n_calls=2
       if budget > 180: n_calls=3
       per=budget/float(n_calls)
       for ci in range(n_calls):
          if time.monotonic() > stage_end - 2: break
          sc,bp,cp = _run_lbfgs(p_cur,beta,per,maxfun_cap,1e-9,1e-12)
          if sc < best_sc:
             best_sc=sc; best_p=bp; print
          cur_score = _score(cp)
          if np.isfinite(cur_score) and cur_score <= best_sc*1.003 + 1e-7:
             p_cur=cp
          else:
             p_cur=best_p
    if time.monotonic() < stage_deadline - 12:
       sc_sg,p_sg=_subgrad_polish(best_p, stage_deadline - 5, 8)
       if sc_sg<best_sc: update
    return best_sc,best_p
```

`_subgrad_polish` used both trajectory and final. It should take max_iters. Ensure deadline not too short.
```
def _subgrad_polish(p0, deadline, max_iters=10):
    p=_normalize(p0); best_p=p; best_sc=_score(p)
    n=len(p); M=2*n-1
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       c=_self_conv(p); d=c*float(n); dmax=float(np.max(d)) if M else 0
       if not finite or dmax<=0: break
       tol=max(1e-7, 1e-5*dmax)
       active=d >= dmax - tol
       cnt=int(active.sum())
       min_active=16
       if cnt < min_active:
          k=min(M, min_active)
          idx=np.argpartition(d, k-1)[-k:]
          active=np.zeros(M, dtype=bool); active[idx]=True; cnt=k
       w=np.zeros(M); w[active]=1.0/float(cnt)
       corr=_cross_corr(p,w)
       gp=4.0*float(n)*corr
       gmax=float(np.max(np.abs(gp))) if n else 0
       if not finite or gmax <=1e-14: break
       best_step=None; best_step_sc=best_sc
       for scale in (0.001,0.005,0.02,0.1,0.5,2.0):
          eta=scale/gmax
          q=p - eta*gp
          q=_project_simplex(q)
          sc=_score(q)
          if sc < best_step_sc:
             best_step_sc=sc; best_step=q.copy()
       if best_step is None: break
       if best_step_sc < best_sc:
          best_sc=best_step_sc; best_p=best_step; p=best_step
       else:
          break
    return best_sc,best_p
```
`np.argpartition(d, k-1)` if k=M? k<=min_active 16, M large >16; okay.

Project simplex:
```
def _project_simplex(v):
    n=v.size
    if n==0: return v
    v=np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    u=np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv=np.cumsum(u)
    rho=np.nonzero(u*(np.arange(1,n+1, dtype=np.float64))-1.0 > 0.0)[0]
    if len(rho)==0:
       return np.zeros(n)
    alphav=(cssv[rho[-1]]-1.0)/float(rho[-1]+1)
    out=np.maximum(v-alphav,0.0)
    s=out.sum()
    if s>0: out/=s
    else: out=np.ones(n)/n
    return out
```
This returns sum1. Good.

Let's write `_run_length_stage` unique selection more concretely.
```
def _run_length_stage(n, stage_deadline, rng, parent, best_p_in, best_sc_in, label):
    now=time.monotonic()
    if stage_deadline - now < 10: return best_sc_in,best_p_in
    if best_p_in is None:
        base_p=_normalize(np.ones(n))
    else:
        base_p=_normalize(_interp(best_p_in,n))
    base_sc=_score(base_p)
    local_best_p = best_p_in if (best_p_in is not None and best_sc_in <= base_sc) else base_p
    local_best_sc = min(best_sc_in, base_sc)

    cands=[('base',base_p)]
    pparent=None
    if parent is not None:
       pparent=_normalize(_interp(parent,n))
       cands.append(('parent',pparent))
    shapes=_shape_candidates(n, rng, 2 if n>=16384 else 4)
    cands.extend(shapes)

    scored=[]
    for name,p in cands:
       p=_normalize(p)
       sc=_score(p)
       if np.isfinite(sc):
          scored.append((sc,name,p))
          if sc<local_best_sc:
             local_best_sc=sc; local_best_p=p.copy()
    # update global-like local if any candidate
    scored.sort()
    traj=[]
    traj.append((base_p,'base'))
    if pparent is not None and not _similar(pparent, base_p):
       traj.append((pparent,'parent'))
    sine=None; other=None; other2=None
    for sc,name,p in scored:
       if name.startswith('sine') and sine is None:
          if not _similar(p,base_p) and (pparent is None or not _similar(p,pparent)):
             sine=p
       if other is None and not name.startswith('sine') and name not in ('base','parent'):
          unique=True
          for q,_ in traj:
             if _similar(p,q): unique=False; break
          if unique: other=p
       if sine is not None and other is not None:
          break
    if sine is not None: traj.append((sine,'sine'))
    if other is not None: traj.append((other,'other'))
    # if still less than 3, add top scored unique
    for sc,name,p in scored:
       if len(traj)>=4: break
       unique=True
       for q,_ in traj:
          if _similar(p,q): unique=False; break
       if unique:
          traj.append((p,name))
          break
    # fractions
    m=len(traj)
    if m==1: fracs=[1.0]
    elif m==2: fracs=[0.7,0.3]
    elif m==3: fracs=[0.6,0.25,0.15]
    else: fracs=[0.55,0.25,0.15,0.05]
    stage_time=stage_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if stage_time <= 0: return local_best_sc, local_best_p
    for i in range(m):
       sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + stage_time * fracs[i]
       if sub_deadline > stage_deadline - 3: sub_deadline=stage_deadline -3
       if time.monotonic() >= sub_deadline: continue
       tag=label+'_'+traj[i][1] if i==0 else label+'_'+traj[i][1]
       sc,pb = _trajectory_lbfgs(traj[i][0], sub_deadline, tag)
       if sc < local_best_sc:
          local_best_sc=sc; local_best_p=pb
    return local_best_sc, local_best_p
```

```python
"""
Search for a non-negative sequence minimizing

    F(a) = 2*n*max(conv(a,a)) / sum(a)^2

using a homotopy of log-sum-exp smoothings optimized in a softmax
parameterization.  The main ingredients are:

- FFT-based convolution and linearized convolution-gradient computation.
- A full log-sum-exp surrogate with increasing beta (50 -> 15000 -> 60000).
- Multi-length progressive search (8192 -> 16384 -> 32768), which often
  reduces finite-length discretization error and exposes a larger attack surface.
- Diverse initializations: the supplied parent when available, asymmetric
  exponential/beta/Gaussian profiles, and several antisymmetric sine-profile
  starts that target the continuous low-convolution regime.
- Final exact max-subgradient polishing in the simplex and small restarts.

The function propose_candidate() is the entry point.
"""

import time
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import minimize


def _next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([target], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 1e-14:
        return np.full(a.size, target / float(a.size), dtype=np.float64)
    return a * (target / s)


def _self_conv(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([a[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    M = 2 * n - 1
    N = _next_pow2(M)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(A * A, N)[:M]
    out = np.nan_to_num(out, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    np.maximum(out, 0.0, out)
    return out


def _score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    S = float(a.sum())
    if S < 1e-14:
        return np.inf
    c = _self_conv(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx < 0:
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * mx / (S * S))


def _cross_corr(a, w):
    """
    Returns h[i] = sum_j w[j] * a[j-i] for i = 0..n-1, where only terms with
    0 <= j-i < n are kept.  This is the half-gradient of sum_j w[j] conv(a,a)[j].
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    m = w.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 0:
        return np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    L = n + m - 1
    N = _next_pow2(L)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a[::-1], N)
    W = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    full = np.fft.irfft(A * W, N)[:L]
    full = np.nan_to_num(full, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    return full[n - 1:n + n]


def _interp(a, m):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    m = int(max(1, m))
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.full(m, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    if n == m:
        return a.copy()
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    q = np.interp(x1, x0, a)
    return np.nan_to_num(q, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)


def _load_parent():
    h = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if h is None:
        return None
    try:
        p = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        return None
    if p.size == 0:
        return None
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, None)
    if float(p.sum()) < 1e-14:
        return None
    return _normalize(p)


def _p_from_y(y, eps):
    y = np.asarray(y, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = y.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    z = y - float(np.max(y))
    ps = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ps.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        ps = np.full(n, 1.0 / n)
    else:
        ps = ps / s
    p = (1.0 - eps) * ps + (eps / float(n))
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0:
        p = np.full(n, 1.0 / n)
    else:
        p = p / s
    return p


def _p_to_y(p, eps):
    p = _normalize(p)
    n = p.size
    base = eps / float(n)
    lower = max(1e-300, 0.5 * base / max(1.0, 1.0 - eps))
    p = np.maximum(p, lower)
    p = p / float(p.sum())
    ps = (p - base) / max(1e-12, 1.0 - eps)
    ps = np.maximum(ps, 1e-300)
    y = np.log(ps)
    y = y - float(np.mean(y))
    return np.nan_to_num(y, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)


def _lse_obj(x, beta, eps, reg):
    """
    Objective and gradient in y-space.
    p = (1-eps)*softmax(y) + eps/n, sum(p)=1.
    Value is 2*LSE_beta(n*conv(p,p)).
    """
    n = int(x.size)
    if n == 0:
        return 1e100, np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    beta = float(beta)
    if beta <= 0:
        beta = 1.0

    z = x - float(np.max(x))
    ps = np.exp(z)
    s = float(ps.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    ps = ps / s
    p = (1.0 - eps) * ps + (eps / float(n))
    p = p / float(p.sum())

    c = _self_conv(p)
    M = c.size
    if M == 0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    d = c * float(n)
    dmax = float(np.max(d))
    if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax <= 0.0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    with np.errstate(under="ignore", over="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        e = np.exp(beta * (d - dmax))
    e = np.nan_to_num(e, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    se = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(se) or se <= 0.0:
        w = np.full(M, 1.0 / M, dtype=np.float64)
        L = dmax
    else:
        w = e / se
        L = dmax + float(np.log(se)) / beta

    val = 2.0 * L

    corr = _cross_corr(p, w)
    gp = 4.0 * float(n) * corr
    gp = np.nan_to_num(gp, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    dot = float(np.dot(ps, gp))
    g = (1.0 - eps) * ps * (gp - dot * ps)

    if reg != 0.0:
        val += float(reg) * float(np.dot(x, x))
        g = g + (2.0 * float(reg)) * x

    g = np.nan_to_num(g, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    mg = float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if n else 0.0
    if np.isfinite(mg) and mg > 1e6:
        g *= 1e6 / mg

    if not np.isfinite(val):
        val = 1e100
        g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)

    return float(val), g


def _run_lbfgs(p0, beta, time_limit, maxfun_cap, eps=1e-9, reg=1e-12):
    p0 = _normalize(p0)
    n = p0.size
    best_p = p0.copy()
    best_sc = _score(p0)
    cur_p = p0.copy()
    cur_sc = best_sc

    time_limit = float(max(0.0, time_limit))
    if n == 0 or time_limit < 4.0:
        return best_sc, best_p, cur_p, cur_sc

    y0 = _p_to_y(p0, eps)
    est = 0.006 * (float(n) / 8192.0) + 0.005
    mf = int(0.6 * time_limit / max(1e-9, est))
    mf = max(50, min(mf, int(maxfun_cap), 6000))
    maxiter = max(20, mf // 3)

    try:
        res = minimize(
            _lse_obj,
            y0,
            method="L-BFGS-B",
            jac=True,
            args=(float(beta), float(eps), float(reg)),
            options={
                "maxiter": maxiter,
                "maxfun": mf,
                "ftol": 1e-16,
                "gtol": 1e-11,
                "maxls": 32,
            }
        )
        x_new = getattr(res, "x", None)
        if x_new is not None and np.all(np.isfinite(x_new)):
            cand = _p_from_y(x_new, eps)
            cand = _normalize(cand)
            cand_sc = _score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(cand_sc):
                cur_p = cand
                cur_sc = cand_sc
                if cand_sc < best_sc:
                    best_sc = cand_sc
                    best_p = cand.copy()

                for s in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75):
                    q = (1.0 - s) * p0 + s * cand
                    q = _normalize(q)
                    qs = _score(q)
                    if np.isfinite(qs) and qs < best_sc:
                        best_sc = qs
                        best_p = q.copy()
            else:
                cur_p = p0
                cur_sc = best_sc
    except Exception:
        cur_p = p0
        cur_sc = best_sc

    return best_sc, best_p, cur_p, cur_sc


def _project_simplex(v):
    n = v.size
    if n == 0:
        return v
    v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.nonzero(u * (np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64) - 1.0) > 0.0)[0]
    if rho.size == 0:
        out = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        alphav = (cssv[rho[-1]] - 1.0) / float(rho[-1] + 1)
        out = np.maximum(v - alphav, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        out = np.full(n, 1.0 / n, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        out = out / s
    return out


def _subgrad_polish(p0, deadline, max_iters=10):
    p = _normalize(p0)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_sc = _score(p)
    n = p.size
    M = 2 * n - 1

    for it in range(max_iters):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        c = _self_conv(p)
        d = c * float(n)
        if d.size == 0:
            break
        dmax = float(np.max(d))
        if not np.isfinite(dmax) or dmax <= 0.0:
            break

        tol = max(1e-7, 1e-5 * dmax)
        active = d >= dmax - tol
        cnt = int(active.sum())
        min_active = 16
        if cnt < min_active:
            k = min(M, min_active)
            if k <= 0:
                break
            idx = np.argpartition(d, k - 1)[-k:]
            active = np.zeros(M, dtype=bool)
            active[idx] = True
            cnt = k

        w = np.zeros(M, dtype=np.float64)
        w[active] = 1.0 / float(cnt)
        corr = _cross_corr(p, w)
        gp = 4.0 * float(n) * corr
        gp = np.nan_to_num(gp, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

        gmax = float(np.max(np.abs(gp))) if gp.size else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(gmax) or gmax <= 1e-14:
            break

        best_step = None
        best_step_sc = best_sc
        for scale in (0.001, 0.005, 0.02, 0.1, 0.5, 2.0):
            eta = scale / gmax
            q = p - eta * gp
            q = _project_simplex(q)
            sc = _score(q)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_step_sc:
                best_step_sc = sc
                best_step = q.copy()

        if best_step is None:
            break
        if best_step_sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = best_step_sc
            best_p = best_step
            p = best_step
        else:
            break

    return best_sc, best_p


def _trajectory_lbfgs(p0, stage_deadline, label):
    p_cur = _normalize(p0)
    best_p = p_cur.copy()
    best_sc = _score(p_cur)
    n = p_cur.size
    t0 = time.monotonic()

    print("    [%s] start n=%d score=%.9f" % (label, n, best_sc), flush=True)

    maxfun_cap = 3000
    if n > 8192:
        maxfun_cap = 1500
    if n > 16384:
        maxfun_cap = 800

    stages = [
        (50.0, 0.08),
        (150.0, 0.10),
        (400.0, 0.12),
        (1000.0, 0.14),
        (2500.0, 0.18),
        (6000.0, 0.20),
        (15000.0, 0.18),
    ]

    available = max(10.0, stage_deadline - t0 - 5.0)
    cum = 0.0
    for beta, frac in stages:
        cum += frac
        stage_end = t0 + cum * available
        if stage_end > stage_deadline - 3:
            stage_end = stage_deadline - 3
        budget = stage_end - time.monotonic()
        if budget < 4.0:
            break

        if budget > 180.0:
            n_calls = 3
        elif budget > 60.0:
            n_calls = 2
        else:
            n_calls = 1
        per = budget / float(n_calls)

        for ci in range(n_calls):
            if time.monotonic() > stage_end - 2.0:
                break
            sc_run, best_run, cur_run, cur_sc_run = _run_lbfgs(
                p_cur, beta, per, maxfun_cap, 1e-9, 1e-12
            )
            if sc_run < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc_run
                best_p = best_run
                print("      [%s] beta=%.0f call=%d: %.9f" % (label, beta, ci, best_sc), flush=True)

            if np.isfinite(cur_sc_run) and cur_sc_run <= best_sc * 1.003 + 1e-7:
                p_cur = _normalize(cur_run)
            else:
                p_cur = best_p.copy()

    if time.monotonic() < stage_deadline - 12.0:
        sc_sg, p_sg = _subgrad_polish(best_p, stage_deadline - 5.0, max_iters=8)
        if sc_sg < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc_sg
            best_p = p_sg
            print("      [%s] subgrad: %.9f" % (label, best_sc), flush=True)

    print("    [%s] end score=%.9f" % (label, best_sc), flush=True)
    return best_sc, best_p


def _similar(a, b, threshold=0.999):
    a = _normalize(a)
    b = _normalize(b)
    if a.size != b.size:
        if a.size < b.size:
            a = _normalize(_interp(a, b.size))
        else:
            b = _normalize(_interp(b, a.size))
    if a.size == 0:
        return False
    d = float(np.dot(a, b))
    na = float(np.linalg.norm(a))
    nb = float(np.linalg.norm(b))
    if na <= 0.0 or nb <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(d):
        return False
    return (d / (na * nb)) > threshold


def _similar_to_any(p, seqs, threshold=0.999):
    for q in seqs:
        if q is not None and _similar(p, q, threshold):
            return True
    return False


def _random_smooth_init(n, rng, strength=1.0):
    z = rng.standard_normal(n)
    N = _next_pow2(n)
    Z = np.fft.rfft(z, N)
    f = np.fft.rfftfreq(N) * N
    cutoff = max(1.0, n / 8.0)
    W = np.exp(-(f / cutoff) ** 2)
    z = np.fft.irfft(Z * W, N)[:n]
    z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    z -= float(z.mean())
    sd = float(z.std())
    if sd > 1e-12:
        z /= sd
    p = np.exp(strength * z)
    return _normalize(p)


def _shape_candidates(n, rng, max_random=4):
    arr = []
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    t2 = 2.0 * np.pi * x
    t4 = 4.0 * np.pi * x
    t6 = 6.0 * np.pi * x

    # Single antisymmetric sine profiles: strong continuous initializers.
    for alpha in (0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 0.9):
        p = 1.0 + alpha * np.sin(t2)
        p = np.clip(p, 1e-10, None)
        arr.append(("sine1_%s" % alpha, _normalize(p)))

    for shift in (-0.2, -0.1, 0.1, 0.2):
        p = 1.0 + 0.7 * np.sin(t2 + 2.0 * np.pi * shift)
        p = np.clip(p, 1e-10, None)
        arr.append(("sinesh_%s" % shift, _normalize(p)))

    # Multi-sine antisymmetric profiles.
    coeff = [
        (0.7, 0.0, 0.0),
        (0.6, 0.25, 0.1),
        (0.5, 0.3, 0.2),
        (0.4, 0.45, 0.25),
        (0.3, 0.55, 0.35),
        (0.5, -0.3, 0.1),
    ]
    for i, (a1, a2, a3) in enumerate(coeff):
        p = 1.0 + a1 * np.sin(t2) + a2 * np.sin(t4) + a3 * np.sin(t6)
        p = np.clip(p, 1e-10, None)
        arr.append(("sinem_%d" % i, _normalize(p)))

    # Asymmetric exponential profiles.
    for lam in (-2.0, -1.5, -1.0, -0.5, 0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0):
        p = np.exp(lam * (x - 0.5))
        arr.append(("exp_%s" % lam, _normalize(p)))

    # Beta-like skew profiles.
    beta_vals = [
        (1.0, 1.0),
        (2.0, 1.0),
        (1.0, 2.0),
        (3.0, 1.0),
        (1.0, 3.0),
        (0.5, 1.0),
        (1.0, 0.5),
        (0.0, 2.0),
        (2.0, 0.0),
        (4.0, 1.0),
        (1.0, 4.0),
    ]
    xb = x + 1e-6
    qb = 1.0 - x + 1e-6
    for i, (aa, bb) in enumerate(beta_vals):
        if aa == 0.0:
            fa = 1.0
        else:
            fa = np.power(xb, aa)
        if bb == 0.0:
            fb = 1.0
        else:
            fb = np.power(qb, bb)
        p = fa * fb
        p = np.clip(p, 1e-10, None)
        arr.append(("beta_%d" % i, _normalize(p)))

    # Asymmetric Gaussians.
    for mu in (0.25, 0.35, 0.65, 0.75):
        for sig in (0.12, 0.2, 0.3):
            p = np.exp(-((x - mu) ** 2) / (2.0 * sig * sig))
            arr.append(("gauss_%s_%s" % (mu, sig), _normalize(p)))

    # Smooth log-normal random profiles.
    for r in range(max_random):
        arr.append(("rand_%d" % r, _random_smooth_init(n, rng, strength=1.0)))

    return arr


def _run_length_stage(n, stage_deadline, rng, parent, best_p_in, best_sc_in, label):
    now = time.monotonic()
    if stage_deadline - now < 12.0:
        return best_sc_in, best_p_in

    if best_p_in is None:
        base_p = _normalize(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    else:
        base_p = _normalize(_interp(best_p_in, n))

    base_sc = _score(base_p)
    if best_p_in is not None and best_sc_in <= base_sc:
        local_best_p = best_p_in
        local_best_sc = best_sc_in
    else:
        local_best_p = base_p.copy()
        local_best_sc = base_sc

    cands = [("base", base_p)]
    pparent = None
    if parent is not None:
        pparent = _normalize(_interp(parent, n))
        cands.append(("parent", pparent))

    max_random = 2 if n >= 16384 else 4
    cands.extend(_shape_candidates(n, rng, max_random=max_random))

    scored = []
    for name, p in cands:
        p = _normalize(p)
        sc = _score(p)
        if np.isfinite(sc):
            scored.append((sc, name, p))
            if sc < local_best_sc:
                local_best_sc = sc
                local_best_p = p.copy()

    scored.sort()

    traj = []
    seen = [base_p]
    traj.append(("base", base_p))

    if pparent is not None and not _similar_to_any(pparent, seen):
        traj.append(("parent", pparent))
        seen.append(pparent)

    sine = None
    other = None
    for sc, name, p in scored:
        if sine is None and name.startswith("sine") and not _similar_to_any(p, seen + [sine]):
            sine = p
        if other is None and not name.startswith("sine") and name not in ("base", "parent"):
            if not _similar_to_any(p, seen + [other]):
                other = p
        if sine is not None and other is not None:
            break

    if sine is not None:
        traj.append(("sine", sine))
        seen.append(sine)
    if other is not None:
        traj.append(("other", other))
        seen.append(other)

    # If still short, add the best scored unique candidate.
    for sc, name, p in scored:
        if len(traj) >= 4:
            break
        if not _similar_to_any(p, seen):
            traj.append((name, p))
            break

    m = len(traj)
    if m == 1:
        fracs = [1.0]
    elif m == 2:
        fracs = [0.7, 0.3]
    elif m == 3:
        fracs = [0.6, 0.25, 0.15]
    else:
        fracs = [0.55, 0.25, 0.15, 0.05]

    stage_time = stage_deadline - time.monotonic()
    if stage_time <= 0.0:
        return local_best_sc, local_best_p

    for i in range(m):
        tag = label + "_" + traj[i][0]
        sub_deadline = time.monotonic() + stage_time * fracs[i]
        if sub_deadline > stage_deadline - 3.0:
            sub_deadline = stage_deadline - 3.0
        if time.monotonic() >= sub_deadline:
            continue
        sc, pb = _trajectory_lbfgs(traj[i][1], sub_deadline, tag)
        if sc < local_best_sc:
            local_best_sc = sc
            local_best_p = pb

    return local_best_sc, local_best_p


def _smooth_perturb(p, rng, strength=0.1):
    p = _normalize(p)
    n = p.size
    N = _next_pow2(n)
    z = np.fft.rfft(rng.standard_normal(n), N)
    f = np.fft.rfftfreq(N) * N
    cutoff = max(1.0, n / 8.0)
    W = np.exp(-(f / cutoff) ** 2)
    noise = np.fft.irfft(z * W, N)[:n]
    noise = np.nan_to_num(noise, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    noise -= float(noise.mean())
    sd = float(noise.std())
    if sd > 1e-12:
        noise = noise / sd
    q = p + strength * noise
    return _normalize(q)


def _zero_small_polish(p):
    p = _normalize(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_sc = _score(p)
    for thr in (1e-14, 1e-12, 1e-10, 1e-8):
        q = p.copy()
        q[q < thr] = 0.0
        if float(q.sum()) <= 0.0:
            continue
        q = q / float(q.sum())
        sc = _score(q)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = sc
            best_p = q
    return best_sc, best_p


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.  Searches over several lengths and returns the best
    non-negative sequence found before the time budget expires.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = float(max(120.0, budget_s))
    overall_end = t_start + total_budget - 60.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    parent = _load_parent()
    MAX_OUT = 32768

    # Fallback best.
    best_p = _normalize(np.ones(8192, dtype=np.float64))
    best_sc = _score(best_p)

    if parent is not None:
        ppar = _normalize(parent)
        if ppar.size > MAX_OUT:
            ppar = _normalize(_interp(ppar, MAX_OUT))
        ppar_sc = _score(ppar)
        if ppar_sc < best_sc:
            best_sc = ppar_sc
            best_p = ppar

    print("Parent: %s, initial best score=%.9f, len=%d" %
          (parent is not None, best_sc, len(best_p)), flush=True)

    # Progressive length plan.  Larger lengths often lower the finite-n penalty.
    if parent is not None:
        plan = [(8192, 0.10), (16384, 0.42), (32768, 0.48)]
    else:
        plan = [(8192, 0.25), (16384, 0.375), (32768, 0.375)]

    total_frac = 0.0
    for _, fr in plan:
        total_frac += fr

    final_budget = max(60.0, 0.18 * (total_budget - 60.0))
    final_start = max(t_start + 300.0, overall_end - final_budget)
    stage_span = max(60.0, final_start - t_start)

    cum = 0.0
    for n, fr in plan:
        cum += fr
        stage_end = t_start + (cum / total_frac) * stage_span
        if stage_end > final_start - 5.0:
            stage_end = final_start - 5.0
        if time.monotonic() > stage_end - 5.0:
            continue

        print("\n=== Length stage n=%d, score so far %.9f ===" % (n, best_sc), flush=True)
        best_sc, best_p = _run_length_stage(
            int(n), stage_end, rng, parent, best_p, best_sc, "L%d" % int(n)
        )

    # Final polish at the currently best length.
    print("\n=== Final polish, score so far %.9f, len=%d ===" % (best_sc, len(best_p)), flush=True)
    if best_p is not None and time.monotonic() < overall_end - 20.0:
        n_best = len(best_p)
        cap = 3000
        if n_best > 8192:
            cap = 1200
        if n_best > 16384:
            cap = 600

        fstart = time.monotonic()
        fend = overall_end - 10.0
        htime = max(10.0, fend - fstart)
        high_plan = [(5000.0, 0.25), (20000.0, 0.30), (60000.0, 0.45)]

        cumh = 0.0
        for beta, frac in high_plan:
            cumh += frac
            sub_end = fstart + cumh * htime
            if sub_end > fend - 3.0:
                sub_end = fend - 3.0
            if time.monotonic() >= sub_end:
                break
            budget = sub_end - time.monotonic()
            if budget < 8.0:
                continue
            sc_run, bp_run, _, _ = _run_lbfgs(best_p, beta, budget, cap, 1e-9, 1e-12)
            if sc_run < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc_run
                best_p = bp_run
                print("  final LSE beta=%.0f: %.9f" % (beta, best_sc), flush=True)

        if time.monotonic() < overall_end - 15.0:
            sc_sg, p_sg = _subgrad_polish(best_p, overall_end - 12.0, max_iters=25)
            if sc_sg < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc_sg
                best_p = p_sg
                print("  final subgrad: %.9f" % best_sc, flush=True)

        # A few smooth restarts to escape shallow local minima.
        remaining = overall_end - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 90.0:
            for r in range(3):
                if time.monotonic() > overall_end - 30.0:
                    break
                p_try = _smooth_perturb(best_p, rng, strength=0.05 + 0.05 * r)
                budget = min(120.0, (overall_end - time.monotonic() - 25.0) / 4.0)
                if budget < 8.0:
                    break
                sc_try, bp_try, _, _ = _run_lbfgs(p_try, 20000.0, budget, cap, 1e-9, 1e-12)
                if sc_try < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc_try
                    best_p = bp_try
                    print("  restart %d: %.9f" % (r, best_sc), flush=True)

        if time.monotonic() < overall_end - 10.0:
            sc0, p0 = _zero_small_polish(best_p)
            if sc0 < best_sc:
                best_sc = sc0
                best_p = p0
                print("  zero-small: %.9f" % best_sc, flush=True)

    out = _normalize(best_p)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if out.size == 0 or float(out.sum()) < 0.01:
        out = np.ones(1024, dtype=np.float64) * 0.5
        out = out / float(out.sum())

    result = [float(x) for x in out]
    print("\nFINAL internal score=%.9f, len=%d, time=%.1fs" %
          (best_sc, len(result), time.monotonic() - t_start), flush=True)
    return result
```
